 President Underwood called the meeting to order at 7:00 p.m. 
In addition to Ms. Underwood, present were Board members 
Edmunds, Fleming, Hauff, Philippart and Pochodylo.  Vice 
President Miletti was absent.  Also present were 
Superintendent Fowler; administrators Bryan, Keeling, 
Machesky, McAvoy, Rajter, Stromayer and Witt; and four 
members of the audience.

PERSONNEL

MHSAA Membership Resolution 
10-070 
It was moved by Mrs. Pochodylo and supported by Mr. Hauff 
that be it therefore resolved that the six (6) secondary schools 
(B) are further enrolled to participate in the approved 
interschool athletic activities sponsored by said 
association. 
The Board of Education/Governing Body hereby delegates to 
the Superintendent or his/her designee(s) the responsibility 
for the supervision and control of said activities and hereby 
accepts the Constitution and By-laws of said association and 
adopts as its own the rules, regulations and interpretations 
(as minimum standards) as published in the current 
HANDBOOK as the governing code under which the said 
schools shall conduct their program of interscholastic 
athletics and agrees to primary enforcement of said rules, 
regulations, interpretations and qualifications.  In addition, it 
is hereby agreed that schools which host or participate in the 
association’s meets and tournaments shall follow and enforce 
all tournament policies, procedures, and schedules. 
This authorization shall be effective from August 1, 2010, and 
shall remain effective until July 31, 2011, during which the 
authorization may not be revoked.

Troy School District, exercising the option permitted by Section 
105c of the State School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public 
Act 300 of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools of 
Choice program for the 2010 -2011 school year for nonresident 
students who attend a school district  in another county if that 
Intermediate School District is contiguous with the Oakland 
Intermediate School District boundaries for the purposes of 
attending the International Academy East program located in 
the Troy School District.

Absent:           Miletti 
meeting for Shelly Carrier, Executive Secretary to the 
Superintendent, who is retiring effective July 31, 2010.    
President Underwood presented Mrs. Carrier with a lovely 
bouquet of flowers and thanked her for her 24 years of service 
to the Troy School District.  Mrs. Carrier said that it has been a 
privilege to work with the Board of Education and everyone 
associated with the Troy School District.   
Dr. Fowler distributed a copy of the district’s Budget 
Salary/Compensation Transparency Report to the Board.  She 
explained that this report has been developed as a result of the 
amended State School Aid Act which requires each school 
district and intermediate school district to post certain 
information on its website within 30 days after a board adopts 
its annual operating budget or any subsequent revision to that 
budget.  The Annual Budget and Transparency Reporting is an 
opportunity to communicate to our community on how we 
utilize the resources that are provided to us.  This information 
will be posted on our website on Thursday, July 15, 2010. 
Dr. Fowler also reported that there are over thirty Bond 
projects in progress throughout the district this summer and 
everything is going smoothly. 
Ms. Philippart questioned Dr. Fowler about status of 20j

2010, we were contacted by RKA  Petroleum Company to 
extend the contract through June 30, 20 11 with the same 
terms.  The contract was  reviewed; and it is recommended 
the contract, based on the lowest profit margin per gallon 
(fixed for the contract period), be extended as stated below.

                      Contract Extension                      
On June 15, 2004, the Board of Education approved a contract for diesel fuel for the 
2004/05 school year.  In early July 2010, we were contacte d by RKA Petroleum Company 
to extend the contract through June 30, 2011 with the same terms.  
Note that this contract is based on the lowest profit margin per gallon (fixed for contract 
period).  Please note that our price lock continues to be lower than t hat obtained by the 
Macomb/Oakland Fuel Consortium.  Our cost per gallon per delivery/profit is $0.0045 for 
regular diesel vs. $0.0147 for the Macomb/Oakland Consortium.  Estimated annual usage 
of 160,000 gallons.  Actual contract will change based on market conditions and fuel 
usage. 
In follow-up with RKA Petroleum, I was told that our pricing is grandfathered under the 
old contract.  Considering that we are being offered a lower price than the Consortium , 
it appears to be in our best interest to continu e this contract. 
Accordingly, I recommend continuation of this bid as follows:  
 RKA Petroleum Company Initial Estimate $330,000 
Note our total cost for diesel fuel in 2009/10 was $268,119.  
Please include this as an action item on the August 17, 2010 Board agenda as an 
expenditure of the 2010/11 General Fund.

continue to do so.  With that in mind, RKA wishes to extend the current fuel contract another 
year, which would take us up to July 31, 2011. 
I would also like to point out that all of the RKA Petroleum Companies are certified minority 
businesses (WBE), which I can supply you with a certificate if necessary.  We would also like 
to discuss the alternative fuel, Biodiesel, with your maintenance facility in the near future.  
RKA Petroleum is a certified BQ9000 Biodiesel Distributer, which is the highest certification 
for quality control through the National Biodiesel Board.  I have enclosed some information 
on this superior product, and we hope that you will consider running this in your fleet this 
school year.

7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice - Section 105c 
In accordance with Michigan  state law, school districts  shall 
pass a resolution each school year if they intend to participate 
in a Schools of Choice program.  It is anticipated that seats will 
be available for the 2011-2012 school year for grades 9 and 10 
at the International Academy East.  It would be appropriate at 
this time to consider adopting a Schools of Choice section 105c 
resolution for ten non-resident 10 th grade students  and an 
unlimited number of 9th grade students who attend a district in 
another county, if that intermediate school district is 
contiguous with the Oakland Intermediate School District 
boundaries.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:  
 Supported by:  
be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy 
School District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105c  of 
the State School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public A ct 300 of 
1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools of Choice 
program for th e 2011-2012 school year for nonresident students 
who attend a school district in another county if that Intermediate 
208
School District is contiguous with the Oakland Inter mediate School 
District boundaries for the purposes of attending the International 
Academy East program located in the Troy School District.

MEMORANDUM 
To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Date: Aug 4, 2010 
Re: Elementary free field microphone replacement  
As discussed in July, the District is required to replace many pieces of wireless microphone equipment due 
to FCC action prohibiting the use of certain radio frequencies that have been in public use for many years.  
Based on conversations with teachers, principals, and administration, this project will replace free field voice 
amplification systems in all lower elementary classrooms (K -2) and provide portable speaker/microphone 
systems to be available via check out for upper elementary.  We do not have sufficient funds to replace every 
system, and usage patterns from the buildings indicate that this hybrid solution is an appropriate 
compromise.  We have access to extra systems as needed where medically indicated.  
The District released bid #9687 on July 16, 2010, with bids opened on August 3.  We received four valid 
responses and one alternate bid for technology that was tested but not approved for th e project.   
The compliant low bid response from VSC, Inc is complete and the vendor has delivered many previous 
projects for the District as requested, and I recommend award to this vendor.  We secured unit pricing on 
various microphones and other compon ents that we will not purchase at this time, so the award amount is 
slightly less than the bid tabulation.   
Please consider the award to VSC as follows:  
Description Amount 
FP-100-R classroom systems $47,190  
REDCAT RC-V portable systems $25,056  
IR Sensors $895  
Shipping $1,500  
Total $74,641  
This item requires Board action due to the dollar amount of the purchase; please place this on the August 17, 
2010 agenda.  Thank you for your attention to this matter.

                      Infrared Wireless Microphones                      
On August 3, 2010 bids were opened for replacement microphones for usage within all 
District elementary schools.  Bids were sent to 21 potentia l bidders with nine responding 
(four of which were “No Bid”).   
Results of this bid have been reviewed with Mr. S. Bryan, Director of Technology, who 
has recommended award to VSC, Inc.  I have also reviewed this bid and concur with 
this recommendation.  Accordingly, I recommend award of this bid as follows:  
   VSC, Inc. $ 74,641.00 
Note:  Award recommendation does not include purchase of (one each) Redmike 
Microphones, controller and cradle.  
Please include this as an action item on the August 17, 2010 Board agenda as an 
expenditure of the 2004 Capital Projects Fund.

8. BUSINESS 
 B.   CONSIDERATION:  SIDEWALK EASEMENT 
 The Troy School District wishes to enter into an agreement with the 
homeowners of the property adjacent to the driveway at Wattles Elementary 
School for the purpose of establishing a permanent easement to extend the 
sidewalk to the school. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below.  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: 
 Supported by: 
 WHEREAS, the Troy School District desires to construct a sidewalk on a portion 
of a parcel of property currently owned by Satyendra and Indira Basu , located in the 
City of Troy, the County of Oakland and the State of Michigan commonly known as 553 
Colebrook, parcel id number 88-20-22-252-036 (the “Property”); and 
 WHEREAS, in order to obtain access to the Property for the purposes stated 
above, the School District desires to obtain a permanent easement from the property 
owners; and 
 WHEREAS, the School District is desirous of authorizing the Superintendent of 
the School District, Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D., to execute an easement agreement on 
terms substantially similar to the terms set forth in the attached Sidewalk Easement 
Agreement. 
 NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT:  The Superintend ent of the 
School District, Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D. is hereby authorized to execute an easement 
agreement with Satyendra and Indira Basu  on terms substantially similar to the terms 
set forth in the attached Sidewalk Easement Agreement. 
AYES: ________   
NAYS: ________

                 8.B.   08.17.10 
SIDEWALK EASEMENT 
Satyendra N. Basu and Indira Basu, whose address is 553 Colebrook, Troy, Michigan 48083-
5110 (collectively, the “ Grantor”), for the consideration of $1.00, the receipt and sufficiency of 
which are acknowledged, grants, warrant and convey to Troy School District, a Michigan 
municipal corporation, whose address is 4400 Livernois, Troy, Mi chigan 48098 -4799 (the 
“School District ”) a permanent easement for the purposes of constructing, installing, using, 
repairing, maintaining, replacing and improving a sidewalk in, over, and across the premises 
lying within the City of Troy, Oakland County, Michigan, and particularly described as:  
The South 82.00 feet of the West 33 feet of the following described parcel: Lot 58 
of "Cloverdale Farms", as recorded in Liber 33, Page 13, Oakland County, 
Michigan records and also that part of the NE Y4 of Section  22, T2N -R11 E, 
lying North of said "Cloverdale Farms" and Westerly and Southerly of "Hidden 
Ridge Subdivision", as recorded in Liber 173, Pages 5 -7, Oakland County, 
Michigan records. Said east line of easement is intended to be the west line of Lot 
58 of said "Cloverdale Farms" extended northerly , 
Tax Parcel Identification #: Part of 88 -20-22-252-036, 
(the “Easement Area”), as set forth on the sketch attached hereto as Exhibit A. 
If it is necessary for work pursuant to this easement for the School District  to enter upon the 
Grantor’s property adjoining the easement, the School District may use that adjoining property.  
However, the School District shall restore the property adjoining the easement to as good or 
better condition than it was prior to any such work. 
Grantor shall not interfere with the School District’s easement rights, and no structure of any 
kind shall be placed in, on or over the Easement Area, except as authorized in writing by the 
School District. 
This easement shall run with the land and b e binding upon the parties hereto and their respective 
grantees, successors and assigns, and shall be executed in recordable form and recorded with the 
Oakland County Register of Deeds.  
This easement is exempt from state and county transfer tax pursuant to  MCL 207.505(a) and 
207.526(a). 
GRANTOR: 
Satyendra N. Basu 
____________________________________  
Indira Basu

 A. CONSIDERATION:  Negotiations 
The Board must pass a resolution to go into closed session.

will go into closed session.  No additional business will take

   Supported by

closed session to discuss negotiations.
